市場焦點
美國
• 10月S&P Global綜合PMI初值由58.4降至57.6，雖較前月回落，但仍處高檔區間；服務業動
能略為放緩，製造業PMI則升至56.0，創近4年半新高。新訂單增速加快，顯示企業需求、投
資及庫存回補動能仍具韌性，然而企業售價與投入成本同步上升，反映需求改善、供應鏈延
遲及成本轉嫁效應持續存在，通膨降溫進程仍具不確定性。整體而言，市場關注焦點轉向通
膨與利率走勢，高利率環境恐維持較長時間，進而推高股債資產評價波動。
中國
• 中國國家統計局公布第三季GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期，在出口與科技製造業帶動下，
短期成長可望維持穩定，但若內需未能有效改善，經濟成長動能恐難全面擴散，建議聚焦在
半導體、AI硬體、伺服器供應鏈，及高端製造相關產業，至於消費、房地產及傳統內需類股，

仍需觀察後續刺激政策與消費信心回升情況。
法國
政局動盪
• 法國金融市場因各地抗議活動持續擴大，面臨股債雙跌的局面。市場憂心法國政府可能擴大支
出以平息抗議，加劇財政可持續性疑慮。法國10年期公債殖利率一度突破5%，法德利差亦擴
大至歐債危機以來高點。不過，歐洲央行系統性壓力綜合指標仍處於歷史相對低檔，遠低於歐
元區主權債務危機期間，短期內演變為新一輪主權債務危機的風險仍相對有限。

美國PMI維持強勁擴張，經濟成長續航但通膨壓力再升溫
• 企業需求強勁支撐美國景氣維持高檔擴張：美國10月綜合PMI初值由58.4降至57.6，雖略低於前月，但仍處於近五年來
的高位區間，顯示企業活動持續高速擴張；其中服務業PMI為57.9、製造業PMI升至56.0並創53個月新高，新訂單增速
更創逾4年半最佳表現，反映內需穩健、出口需求改善與企業補庫存效應同步發酵，美國第四季經濟動能仍具韌性。
• 成本上升與供應瓶頸推升通膨風險：企業售價漲幅創2022年8月以來最高，能源、運輸、金屬原料及薪資成本同步上揚，
加上供應鏈交期延長與訂單積壓擴大，使企業得以持續轉嫁成本，暗示未來數月消費者通膨可能再度偏離聯準會目標區
間，市場對利率維持較高水準的預期恐因此升溫。
• 後市觀察：景氣韌性有利獲利成長，但通膨可能推升殖利率、壓抑高評價資產。投資配置建議優先布局美國大型科技股、

美國金融股及工業資本支出受惠族群，可透過NASDAQ 100 ETF、S&P 500 ETF及美國銀行股ETF參與企業獲利成長；
固定收益則以短天期投資級債為主，以降低利率波動風險。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.10.24
S&P Global PMI 走勢

海外資金流入美國市場，支撐美國資產吸引力
• 海外資金持續流入美國：美國財政部8月TIC資料顯示，
整體資金淨流入1,998億美元，其中民間部門淨流入
1,785億美元，為主要資金來源；外資淨買進美國長
期證券3,007億美元，遠高於美國投資人加碼海外資
產216億美元，反映全球資金在經濟成長與流動性考
量下，對美國金融市場仍維持高度的配置需求。
• 全球資金偏好美國資產：外資8月淨買入美國股票
1,319億美元，高於美國公債與公司債，顯示海外投
資人仍看好美國企業獲利與經濟基本面，雖然短期國
庫券持有量下降，部分跨境美元資金亦出現波動，但
以10月近況來看長天期公債標售仍吸引境外央行、
保險資金及大型資產管理機構參與，反映高殖利率水
準對長期資金仍具吸引力。在經濟成長維持韌性與企

業獲利支撐下，美國資產仍為全球資金配置的重要核
心市場。
• 市場看法：海外資金流入有助反映美國資產吸引力，
惟後續仍須觀察通膨、Fed政策及企業獲利表現。投
資布局建議可以美國投資等級債、美國中短天期公債
及大型AI基礎建設受惠族群為核心配置，兼顧收益、
防禦及長期成長潛力。

歷史數據顯示全球股市突破歷史新高後，後續上漲機率高
• 很多人直覺以為見高就是頂，但過去20年的統計資料顯示，全球股市在創下新高後，多數時間選擇繼續向上走。
• 勝率顯著偏高：全球股市(MSCI全球指數)創新高後持有一年，獲得正報酬的機率高達62%，三個月後的正報酬機率更達
71%。美股與台股表現最亮眼，標普500指數創高後一年上漲機率為76%，那斯達克為79%；而台灣股市表現更為驚人，創
高後三個月內上漲的機率高達82%。
• 下檔拉回風險有限：在創高後短期(一個月內)修正幅度其實相當溫和，全球多數主要市場的回徹幅度中位數僅1%～2%左右。
• 突破前高優於連續創新高：若市場在歷經至少一個月以上的整理後突破前高，其未來一年的平均報酬率高達8.4%，遠高於
連續每天創新高的3.9%。這代表市場經適度整理後的突破，具備更健康的上漲動能。

創新高後的平均遠期報酬率
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.22

顧
市場寬度收窄，由少數巨頭獨撐
• 當前的市場特徵由少數巨頭帶頭衝：科技股權重較高的指數(如那斯達克、台股、韓股)，長期以來都有少數權重股拉動指數
的現象。自2024年以來，全球13個主要市場中有10個市場在創新高時，參與上漲的股票比例明顯下降。已開發市場創新高
時高於200日均線的股票比例從過去75.5%降至65.1%，新興市場更降至61.3%。這意味著指數看似風光，但多數個股並未
同步創高，當漲勢過度集中於少數大型科技巨頭時，整體市場的抗風險能力會相對減弱。
• 小結與投資建議：歷史經驗顯示，創新高本身是強勢市場的特徵，後續繼續走揚的機率遠大於立刻崩盤。然而，鑑於目前市
場寬度收窄、權重高度集中於科技股的現狀，盲目追高個股的風險已顯著增加。投資建議可以利用ETF掌握大盤趨勢，由於

指數上漲主要由龍頭股驅動，透過市值型ETF(如追蹤標普500ETF)能直接享受龍頭巨頭帶來的指數創高紅利，避免選錯個股
而賺了指數、賠了價差。此外，提防短期股市回檔，可運用定期定額分批布局或等待大盤適度拉回整理再行加碼，降低追高
風險。
創新高時不同水準的市場寬度佔比
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.22
已開發市場和新興市場創新高時的寬度

顧
法國政局動盪加劇財政壓力，股債同步承壓
• 法國政局動盪加劇財政壓力，股債同步承壓：法國各地抗議活動持續擴大，最初由學生發起，隨後因生活成本高漲而引發其
他群體響應。在財政空間有限、俄烏戰爭與美伊衝突推升能源價格的背景下，政府難以有效緩解民生成本壓力，法國金融市
場因此面臨股債雙跌的局面。
• 公債殖利率攀升，但系統性風險仍相對可控：市場憂心法國政府可能擴大支出以平息抗議，加劇財政可持續性疑慮。法國10
年期公債殖利率一度突破5%，法德利差亦擴大至歐債危機以來高點。不過，歐洲央行系統性壓力綜合指標（CISS）仍處於
歷史相對低檔，遠低於歐元區主權債務危機期間，顯示金融市場尚未出現廣泛的系統性壓力，短期內演變為新一輪主權債務
危機的風險仍相對有限。
G7社會動盪指數，法國高居榜首

資料來源： Beco models country，2026.10.19 資料來源： Bloomberg，2026.10.19

資料來源： Bloomberg，2026.10.15 資料來源： tradingeconomics，2026.10.21
日本貿易收支
• 日本貿易逆差擴大，進口增幅高於出口：日本2026年9月貿易逆差擴大至5,366億日圓，較去年同期的2,777億日圓增加93%，
高於市場預期的1,810億日圓，連續第五個月呈現貿易赤字。出口年增16.7%至10.97兆日圓，受惠於半導體相關電子零件、
製造設備及汽車出口成長；惟進口年增18.9%至11.51兆日圓，增幅高於出口，主要受到原油、半導體電子零件及液化天然
氣進口增加帶動。其中，原油進口年增51.1%，部分反映日本在荷姆茲海峽局勢緊張下，積極分散能源採購來源。
• 日本央行本週料維持利率不變：日本央行9月將政策利率調升25個基點至1.25%，創31年來新高。考量前幾次升息對經濟的

傳導效果仍待觀察，加上貿易逆差擴大反映進口成本壓力，多數分析師預期日本央行本週將暫停升息，以評估貨幣緊縮對經
濟與通膨的影響。

中國國家統計局公布第三季GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期
• 中國國家統計局公布第三季GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期：顯示中國經濟在第二季落底後呈現溫和回升，從季度表現來看，
一、二、三季增速分別為5.0%、4.3%及4.5%，反映政策支持與外需復甦開始發揮效果，全年達成官方4.5%成長目標的機率
明顯提升，第三季經濟改善的主要動能來自出口，受惠於全球企業持續擴建AI基礎設施，及科技產品需求強勁，中國高科技
產品出口表現亮眼，尤其是積體電路、伺服器、電腦零組件，及儲存設備等產品增幅顯著，高技術製造業維持雙位數成長。
同時，中國對美國及歐盟出口維持強勁，推升貿易順差走升，為支撐經濟的重要力量。然而，內需疲弱仍是當前經濟最大隱
憂，房地產調整尚未結束，居民消費與企業投資意願保守，使經濟復甦結構呈現「外熱內冷」的特徵。

• 展望後市：在出口與科技製造業帶動下，短期成長可望維持穩定，但若內需未能有效改善，經濟成長動能恐難全面擴散，建
議聚焦在半導體、AI硬體、伺服器供應鏈，及高端製造相關產業，至於消費、房地產及傳統內需類股，仍需觀察後續刺激政
策與消費信心回升情況。
資料來源：開源證券，2026.10.22

彭博預估騰訊、阿里巴巴在中國AI競爭中勝出機率較高
• 彭博預估騰訊、阿里巴巴在中國AI競爭中勝出機率較高：彭博產業研究認為，中國AI產業未來將由大型科技平台主導，其中騰
訊、字節跳動與阿里巴巴憑藉龐大用戶基礎、數據資源、資金實力及完整生態系統，在AI競賽中具備明顯優勢，這些企業可將
AI廣泛應用於電商、社群、遊戲、金融與雲端業務，提升營運效率並強化競爭門檻，例如：阿里巴巴利用 AI 驅動電商平台上的
個人化產品推薦，而騰訊則運用 AI 優化短影音廣告並強化微信功能。騰訊自 2026 年 7 月起開始將混元 AI 模型整合至其電子
遊戲中，使遊戲角色及助理能更準確地回應玩家，然而，AI模型訓練與基礎建設投入龐大，獲利能力短期仍受壓抑，且須面對
監管、資料隱私及晶片供應等挑戰。此外，智譜、月之暗面、MiniMax及商湯等AI新創雖具技術創新能力，但缺乏規模效益、

穩定現金流及長期資本支持，未來獲利挑戰較大。
• 投資建議：看好中國AI長期發展者，可優先關注具資金、算力與應用場景優勢的大型平台企業，受惠程度可望高於多數AI新創
公司。

台灣9月外銷訂單再創新高，台股第四季有望先蹲後跳
• 台灣9月外銷訂單再創新高：9月外銷訂單1,142.1億美元，連兩個月破千億美元，再創單月新高；年增率高達62.4%，連續
20個月呈雙位數正成長。經濟部表示，AI強勁需求超乎預期，預估今年第四季接單還會優於第三季。展望10月表現，外銷
訂單將持續亮麗，估落在1,125億至1,159億美元，有望連三個月破千億美元。
• 台股第四季有望先蹲後跳：10月初以來，台股在盤中觸及歷史高點49,968點後面臨一波修正，主要反映在新一輪的財報公
布前，市場觀望AI投資是否存在泡沫化風險。短期來看，台股在季線附近出現支撐，不過在11月初的美國期中選舉落幕前，
台股維持區間震盪的可能性較高；中期來看，美國期中選後往往因政治不確定性下降，歷史統計股市上漲機率高，加上台灣

年底集團作帳及選舉行情，正向看待年底台股表現。

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

Investment case: We initiate coverage of Hon Precision, the global leader 
in test handlers and active thermal control (ATC) systems, with a Buy (1) 
rating. According to management, the company commands >70% market 
share in AI/HPC final test (FT) and c.50% in system-level test (SLT) by 
revenue, distinctly positioning it as one of the key global beneficiaries of the 
rising silicon complexity.

Multi-layer content stacking supports ASP and margin growth. As test 
durations continue to lengthen and thermal requirements rise, customers 
are required to deploy greater parallel testing capacity and higher-
specification handlers, creating a structural demand tailwind for both FT 
and SLT handler. We forecast rising AI chip power consumption and 
package complexity over our forecast horizon to drive adoption of higher-
value ATC systems, mega-tray handlers (adding 25-30% to ASP relative to 
standard dual-temp ATC systems), and AOI integration (adding a further 
10-15% to system ASP), supporting a multi-year increase in content per 
tool and expanding its gross margin from 56.8% to 59.2% over 2026-28E.

Recurring consumables and CPO testing expand long-term growth 
visibility. Cold plates generate high-margin, annuity-like revenue with 
every new chip platform which should benefit from shortening AI processor 
refresh cycles. Beyond consumables, Hon Precision is extending its testing 
platform into co-packaged optics (CPO), where optical-electrical co-testing 
could unlock a new addressable market beyond traditional GPU and ASIC 
testing. While near-term contribution remains limited, we view CPO as a 
strategic growth option that enhances the company’s long-term earnings 
visibility and valuation premium over our forecast horizon.

Catalysts: 1) capacity expansion ramp in 2027; 2) mega-tray penetration 
and higher-power ATC adoption; 3) initial CPO test solution qualification.

Valuation: We initiate with a Buy (1) rating and 12M TP of TWD6,365, 
based on a target PER of 30x applied on our 2027E EPS. The stock 
currently trades at c.19x forward PER, despite our projected 2026-28E EPS 
CAGR of 55%, implying a PEG of only 0.35x. We believe its current 
valuation heavily discounts macro noise with share price pulling back by 
c.53% from its historical high and derating from a cycle-peak multiple of 45-
50x 1-year-forward PER. Backed by solid order visibility through late-2027, 
we highlight Hon Precision’s risk-reward profile as decisively asymmetric.

Risks: 1) Slower-than-expected AI/HPC investment; 2) delayed ramp-up of 
new products; and 3) potential loss of market share.

10 November 2026

Hon Precision (7769 TT): 10 November 2026

How do we justify our view? 
Growth outlook Valuation Earnings revisions

Growth outlook

Hon Precision: revenue and gross margin outlook 
We expect Hon Precision’s growth momentum over 2026-
28E to be driven by increasing testing content per chip, 
supported by rising power consumption in AI/HPC chips, 
larger package sizes, and growing system complexity. We 
expect the transition towards high-end ATC handlers, 
mega-tray solutions, and integrated AOI functions to drive 
meaningful ASP expansion over our forecast horizon, with 
recurring cold plate demand providing an additional gross-
margin growth driver.

Source: Company, Daiwa research

Valuation   Hon Precision: 1-year-forward PER bands 
Our 12-month TP of TWD6,365 is based on a target PER 
of 30x applied to our 2027E EPS forecast. Following the 
strong share-price rally in 1H26, investor focus shifted 
away from Hon Precision due to limited near-term catalysts 
ahead of the 2H27 ramp-up of mega-tray handlers, CPO 
testing solutions and new capacity. We believe the recent 
53% pullback from its historical high on 29 May 2026 and 
derating from 45-50x 1-year-forward PER to c.19x more 
than prices in the macro uncertainties. At the current levels, 
the stock offers an attractive risk-reward profile, trading at 
a PEG of just 0.35x based on our 2026-28E EPS CAGR of 
55%.

Source: Bloomberg

Earnings revisions

Hon Precision: Bloomberg-consensus earnings revisions 
The Bloomberg consensus raised its 2026E and 2027E 
EPS estimates by 68% and 122%, respectively, over the 
past year, driven by stronger-than-expected AI-related 
demand and improving visibility on the company’s long-
term growth trajectory. We believe the upward revisions 
reflect rising confidence in volume and ASP expansion 
across the company’s key testing solutions, supported by 
accelerating AI infrastructure investments and expanding 
customer adoption.

Source: Bloomberg

Company profile 
Founded in 1999, Hon Precision is the global leader in semiconductor test handlers and active thermal 
control (ATC) systems. The company dominates the global AI/HPC final test handler market, supported 
by its advanced thermal management technologies. With over 70% market share in AI/HPC test 
handling, Hon Precision benefits from growing demand for AI, HPC and custom ASIC applications. Its 
solutions are widely adopted by leading OSATs and foundries worldwide, making it a key enabler of 
advanced semiconductor testing.

### Page 6

6

Hon Precision (7769 TT): 10 November 2026 
Growth Catalysts 
Multi-factor dollar content stacking 
We believe the primary growth catalyst for Hon Precision over our forecast horizon stems 
from multi-layered dollar content stacking, where the rise in machine unit value should 
outpace volume growth. Next-gen AI accelerators have historically exhibited more than 50-
70% increase in thermal design power (TDP) per generation, with platforms like Nvidia 
Rubin and Rubin Ultra surging to 2.3kW and 3.6kW vs. Blackwell’s 1.2kW to 1.4kW. 
Managing these extreme thermal loads makes high-end active thermal control (ATC) a 
necessity, driving a structural step-up in ASPs from standard handlers at approximately 
TWD6m to dual-temperature ATC systems at TWD12-15m, and tri-temperature ATC 
systems at TWD15-18m.

Concurrently, as the package size of next-generation ASICs/GPUs increases from the 
current 120mm tray specification to around 150mm, Hon Precision is introducing mega-
tray large-package solutions in October 2026, which command a 25-30% ASP premium 
over mainstream GPU handlers. As the majority of next-generation ASIC/GPU is set to 
exceed the boundary of 120mm, we estimate shipment penetration for mega-tray solutions 
will likely reach 40% in 2027E. Integrated functions of Automated Optical Inspection (AOI) 
will contribute an additional 10-15% ASP uplift to unit value of handler. Furthermore, this 
content expansion extends into the flagship mobile AP market as smartphone processors 
transition to multi-die packaging. For example, Apple’s A20 Pro processor has already 
adopted wafer-level multi-chip module (WMCM) packaging in 2026, requiring high-end ATC 
systems capable of managing up to 3,000W thermal dissipation to resolve device self-
heating.

Hon Precision: ASP multipliers

Source: Daiwa research

High-margin cold plate consumables  
In addition to capital equipment set sales, Hon Precision’s cold plate consumables 
business serves as a crucial recurring revenue engine and a primary driver of gross margin 
expansion. Cold plates carry the highest gross margin across all company product lines. 
Driven by the compression of AI processor lifecycles to 1-1.5 years (down from 2-3 years 
historically), each test handler requires 5-10 distinct, custom-engineered cold plates 
tailored to specific die footprints, socket layouts, and thermal hot-spot geometries. To 
conduct parallel test and maintain the throughput of chip testing, test handler for system 
level test (SLT) requires even more cold plates, which can go up to 24 plates. As a result, 
cold plate tooling generates high-frequency annuity-like sales that recur with every new 
chip platform iteration, independent of major equipment replacements.

Hon Precision: cold plate revenue trend

Testing duration and volume multiples 
We believe the rapid rise in AI chip complexity is driving structural growth in semiconductor 
testing intensity, positioning Hon Precision as a key global beneficiary through its 
leadership in high-parallelism test handlers. Driven by the mainstream adoption of 
heterogeneous integration (such as TSMC’s CoWoS and Intel’s EMIB), verifying complex 
AI silicon has extended test durations by 2x to 10x vs. standard consumer chips (5 to 10 
seconds). Final test durations for AI GPUs have expanded from hundreds of seconds to 
over 10 minutes per chip. To maintain daily manufacturing throughput despite this 10x 
spike, chipmakers must scale up massive parallel testing architectures and procure 
significantly more handlers, cementing Hon Precision as the primary test handler partner 
for tier-one US AI GPU and CPU players.

Concurrently, escalating chip complexity, TDP, and die costs make field return (RMA) 
penalties far exceed testing expenses, forcing cloud service providers (CSPs) and 
automotive OEMs to mandate system-level testing (SLT). Because SLT executes over 
extended durations ranging from minutes to hours per chip, high-volume production 
mandates automated parallel SLT handlers equipped with high-capacity ATC. Holding 
c.50% market share in AI/HPC SLT handlers alongside Chroma, Hon Precision is 
expanding beyond GPUs into custom CSP ASICs (eg, Google TPU and AWS) and server 
CPUs. As SLT adoption broadens across non-GPU devices, Hon Precision leverages this 
multi-year runway to transition into a full-spectrum semiconductor testing platform.

Global: growing chip complexity – testing time by AI/HPC chip

Source: Daiwa research

CPO unlocks new TAM beyond GPUs 
Beyond conventional electrical testing, Co-Packaged Optics (CPO) represents a multi-year 
paradigm shift towards a multimodal optical-electrical testing platform. Hon Precision 
currently monetises initial CPO deployments by supplying Final Test (FT) handlers for pure 
electrical testing (E/E test) of switch ASICs and Optical Engines (OEs) on production 
platforms such as Nvidia’s Spectrum-X and Quantum-X.

Hon Precision (7769 TT): 10 November 2026

However, the strategic growth catalyst is anchored in next-generation Insertion 4 (package-
level optical and electrical co-testing, O/E co-test). Because an undetected defect in a 
single optical component would force manufacturers to scrap an entire high-value CPO 
module, early and rigorous package-level validation is imperative for yield protection. 
Collaborating directly with ATE leader Teradyne and optical instrument specialists (such as 
Keysight), Hon Precision is developing a specialised ATC handler capable of simultaneous 
optical and electrical validation, including Bit Error Rate (BER), optical loopback, crosstalk, 
and high-power thermal management testing for integrated ASIC and Optical Engine 
modules in a single touchdown.

Engaged with Nvidia CPO switch programmes and actively expanding to additional ASIC 
clients, Hon Precision targets engineering verification in late-2026 and initial shipments in 
2027, ahead of 2028 volume production. Although we expect revenue contribution will 
likely be relatively low (<5% in 2027-2028), it drives valuation and market expectation to 
serve larger total addressable market (TAM).

Market Analysis 
Global testing equipment market 
According to TechInsights, global testing equipment capex is projected to expand 
significantly from USD8.3bn in 2024 to USD26.1bn by 2027E, representing a 46.4% 
CAGR. Notably, while SoC test systems front-loaded strong growth in 2024-25, before 
tapering to 9.6% YoY in 2027E on TechInsights estimates, package handling equipment is 
forecasted to exhibit strong structural resilience and longevity. Based on TechInsights, 
market capex for package handling equipment will likely more than double from USD941m 
in 2024 to USD2.6bn by 2027E (40.9% CAGR), re-accelerating to 46% YoY growth in 
2027E from 34% YoY in 2025. This cadence reflects the phased deployment of test 
infrastructure – initial tester investments are followed by high-volume back-end handling 
installations as advanced packaging (eg, CoWoS, Chiplets) scales into mass production.

Global semi testing equipment: capex  Global semi testing equipment: handler and prober growth

Source: TechInsights, Daiwa research  Source: TechInsights, Daiwa research

Handler market players 
Hon Precision’s overall share in the global test handler TAM rose from 35% in 2024 to 57% 
in 2025, and we forecast this to reach 75% in 2026E and 79% by 2027E. Within the high-
value AI and HPC Final Test (FT) handler segment, Hon Precision maintains a dominant 
market share, exceeding 70% in both sales value and shipment volume, per management. 
In the rapidly expanding AI and HPC SLT handler segment, Hon Precision holds c.50% 
market share in terms of revenue, per management, alongside its primary peer, Chroma, 
establishing a duopoly in global high-end SLT testing.

Hon Precision: handler market share forecasts

Source: Daiwa research

and regional vendors, including Cohu (COHU US, NR), Advantest (6857 JP, JPY28,375, 
Outperform [2]; covered by Daiwa analyst Takeshi Fukuyama), and mainland Chinese 
suppliers such as Changchuan Technology (300604 CH, NR) and JHT Design (603061

### Page 10

10

Hon Precision (7769 TT): 10 November 2026

While global incumbents rely on brand prestige, standard architectures, and bundled 
offerings, Hon Precision excels in leading-edge AI/HPC testing through its ultra-high-power 
Active Thermal Control (ATC) and rapid engineering turnaround. As multi-kilowatt dynamic 
loads (1.5kW–3kW+) and extreme warpage on >120mm packages push standard handlers 
beyond their limits, Hon Precision’s hyper-customised ATC fluidics, high-force micro-
mechanics, and modular designs deliver superior millisecond thermal clamping and agile 
package adaptation. Furthermore, while giants like Advantest dominate the SoC ATE tester 
market (>55% share), their in-house handlers focus primarily on memory and standard 
logic. Consequently, tier-one chipmakers and OSATs standardly decouple tester and 
handler sourcing, thus pairing Advantest or Teradyne testers with Hon Precision’s high-
power ATC handlers. Located directly within Taiwan’s advanced packaging cluster, Hon 
Precision provides real-time, on-site co-validation and custom cold-plate engineering that 
preserve formidable entry barriers against bundled solutions.

Meanwhile, although Chinese competitors have expanded aggressively in domestic mature 
nodes and conventional Pick-and-Place handlers, Hon Precision commands a decisive 
generational lead in high-complexity advanced packaging applications. Chinese vendors 
remain largely confined to lower-wattage consumer, automotive, and industrial IC testing, 
lacking the proven patents, structural rigidity, and micro-level force balancing required to 
safely apply hundreds of kilograms of contact force onto fragile, multi-chip CoWoS 
packages without cracking dies or warping substrates. Moreover, because enterprise AI 
chips cost tens of thousands of dollars each, tier-1 global fabless leaders and North 
American cloud service providers (CSPs) maintain virtually zero tolerance for testing 
failure, establishing an entrenched validation moat for Hon Precision. Free from 
geopolitical export constraints, Hon Precision can seamlessly deploy across global OSAT 
networks in Taiwan, North America, and Southeast Asia.

Financial Analysis 
AI demand to sustain strong revenue growth 
In 2025, Hon Precision’s top line exceeded TWD30bn (+116% YoY), driven by global AI 
cluster rollouts. We forecast Hon Precision to sustain its top-line growth momentum over 
2026-28E. We expect this to be mainly driven by core test handler and ATC systems (70-
75% of revenue), where we forecast capacity to expand 40% YoY in 2026E, and 50% YoY 
over 2027-28E with utilisation rate remaining above 90%. We forecast blended ASP of test 
handler ASP to rise from TWD14.2m in 2025 to TWD16.3m by 2028E, mainly driven by 
increased adoption rate of mega tray (+20-25% ASP) and AOI (+10-15%) system. High-
margin cold plate consumables revenue should also benefit from test handler shipment, 
which we forecast to increase at a 66.1% CAGR over 2025-28E.

Balance sheet and cash flow 
Driven by surging AI/HPC testing demand, Hon Precision is actively expanding its capacity 
across the new Desheng facility, localised production at the Changshu site, and 
debottlenecking of headquarter efficiency. The company targets 40-50% capacity growth in 
2026 and another 50% increase in 2027. The expansion of manufacturing space and 
production facilities requires an increase in capital expenditure, which we project Hon 
Precision will ramp up to approximately TWD2.8bn in 2026E, TWD3.8bn in 2027E, and 
TWD4.6bn in 2028E. The company’s operating cash inflows are more than sufficient to 
fully fund factory expansions alongside a c.70% dividend payout ratio.

Hon Precision continues to maintain an un-levered balance sheet with zero financial bank 
debt. Cash and cash equivalents exceeded TWD53bn in 2025; we project this figure to 
surpass TWD75bn by 2028E. Its robust balance sheet underpins peer-leading shareholder 
returns, with ROE to expand from 34.5% in 2025 to 59.6% by 2028E. This self-funding 
cash engine supports management’s targeted c.70% dividend payout ratio.

Valuation and recommendation 
Initiating coverage with a Buy (1) rating 
We initiate coverage of Hon Precision with a Buy (1) rating and 12-month TP of TWD6,365, 
based on a target PER of 30x applied to our 2027E EPS forecast. When the stock rallied 
above TWD8,000 in 1H26, its forward PER multiple stretched to a cycle peak of 45-50x. 
However, we expect key growth catalysts, including a 50% aggregate capacity expansion, 
alongside higher-ASP shipments of large-form-factor mega tray handlers and co-packaged 
optics (CPO) test solutions, to drive its top line, primarily in 2H27. With existing production 
lines operating at full utilisation, investors are concerned over a potential temporary 
deceleration in growth momentum through 2H26-1H27, ahead of the next potential major 
capacity inflection in 2H27.

However, as the calendar rolls into 2027, we believe market attention will swiftly refocus on 
the company’s multi-year structural thesis. Easing capacity bottlenecks, combined with 
robust ASIC-driven adoption of mega tray architectures and ramping CPO test platforms, 
should spark a powerful volume-and-ASP expansion cycle. The stock is currently trading at 
a 19x 1-year-forward PER vs. its earnings CAGR of 55% over 2026-28E, translating into a 
0.35x PEG ratio. We believe the valuation now heavily discounts the near-term pause in 
revenue and macro noises, presenting a compelling risk-reward profile for long-term 
investors.

Risks to our call 
1. Slower-than-expected AI/HPC investment: A slowdown in AI infrastructure spending 
or delays in GPU, ASIC and server CPU ramps could reduce demand for high-end 
FT/SLT handlers and thermal-control solutions, weakening order growth and revenue 
visibility. 
2. Slower-than-expected new product adoption: Customer qualification delays or 
slower adoption of mega-tray handlers, AOI inspection systems and CPO FT solutions 
could delay product ramps, resulting in revenue growth below our expectations. 
3. Potential share loss: Competitors’ advances in thermal control, customer qualification 
of alternative suppliers or delays in meeting new testing requirements could weaken 
Hon Precision’s position on next-generation platforms, affecting market share and 
pricing power.

### Page 15

Hon Precision (7769 TT): 10 November 2026 
Appendix 
Company introduction 
Hon Precision was founded in Taichung in 2015, building on the technology and industry 
experience of Hon Technology, which was established in 1999. The company develops 
semiconductor test handlers and thermal-control solutions for AI GPUs, AI ASICs, server 
CPUs, automotive chips and consumer electronics, supporting customers from new-
product introduction (NPI) through volume production.

Hon Precision has two main product categories:

• Semiconductor test handlers: Final-test (FT) and system-level-test (SLT) handlers 
automate chip loading, positioning and sorting. These platforms can incorporate active 
thermal control (ATC) systems to maintain chips at specified temperatures during 
testing. 
• Testing fixtures and modules: Mainly customised cold plates and socket layout kits 
(SLKs), which provide thermal management and mechanical interfaces for different chip 
packages. Customers may require replacement cold plates and kits as package designs 
change, even when retaining existing handlers.

Hon Precision’s order mix is significantly weighted towards AI/HPC/ASIC applications, 
which accounted for 78% of orders in 1Q-3Q26, up from 72% in 2025. These applications 
include AI computing, servers and networking. Automotive represented 9% of orders, 
followed by mobile AP and communications at 7%, consumer electronics at 4%, and 
memory and MEMS at 2%.

As for capacity, Hon Precision’s manufacturing footprint is primarily based in Taiwan, with 
Taichung serving as the group’s main R&D, engineering, quality management, and 
equipment production hub. In addition, the company operates a Suzhou facility focused on 
manufacturing cold plates and related kits, while Taichung Plant 4 is expected to provide 
incremental production capacity to support future growth.

Shareholder structure 
Hon Precision’s major shareholders include several investment and development 
companies. As of 17 April 2026, Hung Min Development was the largest shareholder with a 
17.37% stake, followed by Hongji Investment at 12.08% and Hong Yu Development at 
7.80%. Chairman Wen-Ta Hsieh also directly held 1.77%. International institutional 
shareholders included Norges Bank and GIC, with disclosed stakes of 1.30% and 0.97%, 
respectively. The company has a free float of 48%, allowing broad public-market 
participation and supporting trading liquidity.

### Page 17

Hon Precision (7769 TT): 10 November 2026 
Appendix 
What is handler? 
A semiconductor test handler automates the movement, positioning and temperature 
conditioning of packaged chips during testing. It places each device into a test socket, 
applies the required contact force and sorts the device according to the test results. The 
tester performs the electrical measurements, while the handler ensures consistent 
mechanical and thermal conditions.

The process begins with packaged chips loaded into the handler in trays. After temperature 
conditioning, the handler transfers each chip into a test socket connected to the tester 
through a load board. Once electrical contact is established, the tester applies signals and 
measures the chip’s response. For ATC-equipped systems, a customised cold plate works 
with the active thermal control system to maintain the required chip temperature 
throughout testing. The handler then unloads and sorts the chips based on their test 
results.

Semiconductor test handler: workflow and thermal control system

Source: Hon Precision, Daiwa research

Handlers are used in both final test (FT) and system-level test (SLT). FT handlers work 
with automated test equipment (ATE) to check electrical characteristics and functionality 
against specifications. SLT handlers place chips into test positions connected to system-
level platforms, typically comprising test boards, test PCs and power supplies, to evaluate 
operation under conditions resembling actual use.

### Page 18

18

Hon Precision (7769 TT): 10 November 2026 
Semiconductor back-end testing FT workflow

Source: Semiconductor engineering

Thermal control becomes increasingly important as AI/HPC chips consume more power 
and adopt larger, more complex packages. Different test patterns change power 
consumption and heat distribution, requiring dynamic heating or cooling to keep the chip 
within a specified temperature range. Precise handling and temperature control support 
repeatable measurements, while parallel testing and efficient chip movement help improve 
overall test throughput.

ZDT 3Q26 core business (OPI) was 10%/11% higher than GSe and 
BBG consensus, mainly thanks to the better than expected product 
mix (high margin & high growth sectors accounted for 27% of total 
revenue in 3Q26 vs. our estimate only at 20-25%). In 3Q26, ZDT GM 
was 1.8/2.6ppt higher than GSe/BBG consensus, and was 27.7% up 
4.6ppt QoQ and 5.7ppt YoY, while the annual growth was only 
driven by the better product mix/ new business contribution 
(ABF/optical module/AI PCB), even considering these new 
businesses only accounted for 27% of total revenue.  
OPEX in 3Q26 increased 24% QoQ and 56% YoY due mainly to the 
higher investment in the R&D projects for future new high-end AI 
PCB/ optical transceiver / substrates business, which was the main 
reason for the company to see a 0.7/1.4ppt higher than GSe/BBG 
consensus OPEX to sales ratio in 3Q26.  
In 4Q26, we expect ZDT revenue to go up by ~29% QoQ, mainly due 
to the solid growth from the optical transceiver, substrates, and AI 
PCB businesses, with more new AI projects to ramp in 4Q26. 
Moreover, we expect the company’s GM to further expand to ~30% 
(2.2ppt QoQ or 7.3ppt YoY) thanks to a better product mix (we 
expect high growth/high margin new business will account for 
30-35% of the company’s total revenue in 4Q26, whose average GM 
should be at least 40-45% in 4Q26).  
Investment view 
We remain positive on ZDT’s AI business outlook, and expect the 
acceleration on ZDT’s AI PCB/Substrates business revenue to further 
drive the company’s overall GM level in coming years (we expect the 
two businesses’ average GM could surpass 50% in 2027, thanks to 
the favorable supply demand condition) while we also expect the 
high GM optical transceiver (50%+ GM now) will also see at least 
120% YoY growth in 2027, suggesting a significant GM expansion 
trend for the overall company in coming quarters/years.

### Page 3

We believe ZDT will continue to be the key share gainer in the AI PCB market. For AI GPU 
projects, we are seeing ZDT share increase from <5% in 2025 to ~10-15% in 2026, 
which, we believe, will further go up to 30%+ in long term (2028 and beyond), thanks to 
the company’s proactive investment in the R&D on the latest PCB technology, as well as 
the company’s solid investment in the AI grade PCB capacity. For AI ASIC projects, the 
company’s market share is well below 10% for now, and only focus on some minor 
projects, but we do expect the company to become one of the major suppliers for AI 
ASIC projects in coming years, considering the company’s more diversified production 
sites (mainland China + Taiwan + Thailand), while most of the AI ASIC customers are 
asking for non-Greater China capacity now.  
For the optical transceiver business, we expect ZDT to see strong growth (50%+ 
2025-29E CAGR), driven mainly by the ~50% 2026-28E volume demand CAGR and the 
company’s proactive expansion in capacity in coming years (5x more capacity over the 
next two years). Moreover, ZDT currently holds an industry-leading yield of 90%+ for its 
optical module mSAP products (vs. peers only at 60-80% per our industry checks).  
For its ABF substrate business, we believe the company will continue to have the ability 
to increase ABF price, supported by full UTR and strong demand, while the industry in 
our view remains conservative on its capacity expansion plans over the next 1-2 years 
relative to our forecasts. We expect ABF substrate LTA/Spot prices to increase by 
10-15%/20%+ QoQ per quarter from 3Q26 through at least the end of 2027, with 
shortages likely extending into 2028 (see here). Overall, the business condition for ABF 
substrate is improving with price increase and we expect the business, which was loss 
making in 2025, to expand its GM to 40%+/50%+ by end of 2026/27. 
Overall, we reiterate our Buy rating on ZDT, with a new 12m TP of NT$1,070 (from 
NT$1,025).

Earnings revisions 
We revise up our 2026/27/28/29E net income estimate by 6/21/42/49% after seeing 
the solid GM expansion from these high-growth sectors, which we believe will continue 
to be the net income driver in the coming years. We believe the high growth sectors (AI 
PCB, substrates, optical module mSAP) will account for 50%+ of total revenue in 2028 
and further expand to 60%+ in 2029, with a much higher than company average GM 
(50%+ in average in 2027-28), thus we revise up our 2027/28/29E GM by 
5.4/10.7/12.4ppt vs. previous estimate.

### Page 4

Valuation 
We revise up ZDT 12m TP from NT$1,025 to NT$1,070, still based on our SOTP based 
valuation methodology of (1) 3.5x blended P/B for ABF & BT substrate (in-line with 
upcycle industry average), (2) 15x P/E for high-growth AI server/switch related PCB (in 
line with industry average), and (3) 8x P/E for low-growth consumer electronics PCB 
(in-line with industry downcycle average valuation), and maintain our valuation period at 
4Q26-3Q27, factoring in our earnings revision.

Zhen Ding Technology (ZDT) is the global leading PCB supplier (top 1 in market share in 
sales) with 7-10% market share, and started its ABF substrate business from 2023 in 
Shenzhen, China, and has the highest ABF revenue exposure to the China market 
compared to all other ABF substrate suppliers. We believe ZDT will hold more than 40% 
ABF substrate market share in China by 2027E (up from 31% in 2024) and benefit the 
most from the strong and growing China ABF substrate market (53% 2025E-27E CAGR) 
due to the increasing in-sourcing ratio for the AI computing ASIC/GPU market in China. 
We also expect the increasing foldable smartphone penetration rate in the long term to 
drive ZDT’s FPC TAM, and account for 50% of the company’s total revenue in 2027E. We 
see further revenue upside potential from foldable phones. We are Buy rated mainly due 
to our positive view on the company’s China ABF substrate business opportunity, which 
should not only drive ZDT’s revenue growth, but also improve its OPM level in the long 
term. We see upside for the stock price considering the company’s solid growth 
opportunity, and our TP implied P/E multiple is still lower than the company’s past 10+ 
year P/E average, which we believe is fair considering the potential upside from China 
ABF substrate market and increasing foldable phone penetration rate.

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We update our view after 3Q26 results (issued 6 November).

What’s the impact: Fundamentals largely on track. Rising CPU demand 
from agentic AI has lifted our 2026 regular server growth target to >50% 
YoY (vs. 20-30% YoY), making regular servers the key growth driver for 
Wiwynn and helping it offset the slower-than-expected AMZN ASIC AI 
server ramp in 3Q26 (ramping from September vs. original July estimate). 
Looking ahead, we expect the T3 lifecycle to likely peak in 2Q/3Q27, but 
the rising contribution from both Nvidia and AMD GPU AI servers should 
offset the slowdown in 2H27. As for the margin trend, we expect regular 
servers to be at 10% and ASIC AI servers to be at 8%. However, we expect 
the rising GPU AI server to gradually dilute the blended gross margin as 
Wiwynn focuses only on L11, while GPU motherboard (L6) and tray level 
(L10) will be handled by its parent company Wistron (3231 TT, TWD171, 
Buy [1]). As such, we expect the gross margin for Wiwynn’s GPU AI servers 
to be 2-3%, lower than the 4-5% AI of other ODM peers.

3Q26 results beat expectations. Wiwynn reported 3Q26 net income of 
TWD23.36bn (EPS: TWD41.46), beating our expectation by 8%. 3Q26 
sales (TWD434.62bn; up 56% QoQ and 63% YoY) beat our estimate by 6% 
and the Bloomberg consensus estimate by 7% on stronger-than-expected 
regular server shipments. Regular servers accounted for 50-55% of its 
revenue, down from 60-70% in 2Q26, while AI servers contributed 45-50%. 
Gross margin declined by 0.4pp QoQ to 8.9%, but still exceeded our 8.2% 
estimate. Management attributed the sequential margin contraction mainly 
to lower NRE recognition and rising material costs, as not all memory 
components used in regular servers are procured under consignment 
arrangements. However, we believe lower regular server contribution to the 
product mix was key reason. With better operating leverage, operating 
margin improved by 0.2pp QoQ to 7.5%. That said, net profit was dragged 
down by higher interest expense (reaching TWD3bn in 3Q26 vs. TWD2bn 
in 2Q26) and a higher tax rate of 23.2% (vs. 21.1% in 2Q26).

What we recommend: We lift our 2026-27E EPS by 1-5% to reflect 
stronger regular server demand but cut 2028E EPS by 3% to reflect higher 
interest expense assumption. We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating but trim our 
12M TP to TWD2,500 (from TWD2,715), based on a target PER of 13x 
(prior: 18x; within its past-2-year trading range of 9-21x) applied to our 1-
year forward diluted EPS, given weaker market sentiment. Key downside 
risks: weaker-than-expected server demand and CSP capex.

How we differ: Our 2028E EPS is 3% above street estimates due to our 
higher regular and AI server shipment assumptions.

11 November 2026

3Q26 review: fundamentals largely on track

➢ Regular server strength sustaining into 2H26, thanks to agentic AI 
➢ Increasing AI server projects from 4Q26 to boost revenue momentum 
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) rating; trimming 12-month TP to TWD2,500

Company profile 
Established in 2012 and listed in 2019, Wiwynn (a subsidiary of Wistron Corp) is a dedicated ODM-
direct supplier of servers for hyperscale datacentres, with major customers including Facebook, 
Microsoft, and Amazon. Aside from hyperscaler customers, the company is expanding its target markets 
to what it terms "Next Wave" customers, including tier-2 cloud service providers and telecom operators, 
which could bring growth opportunities related to edge computing and open RAN.

### Page 5

5

Wiwynn Corp (6669 TT): 11 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 11 Nov 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
Wiwynn plans to have seven to nine directors on its board who will serve three-year terms in
accordance with the Articles of Incorporation in Taiwan. Currently, Wiwynn has 9 board
members with five independent directors. The background of independent directors is diversified
and their competence includes technology, business management, and law. We deem the
diversified background as a positive factor for the company’s board quality. Wiwynn held 9 board
meetings in 2025 and the average attendance rate was around 99%.
The Chairman does not serve concurrently as the President or CEO, which we think is a positive
for board and executive management as well as external shareholder interest.
Capital management 2
Wiwynn declared cash DPS of TWD145 and a stock dividend of TWD20 per share for 2025,
representing a total payout ratio of 60.0%. We note that investors favour its high dividend payout
ratio policy. However, Wiwynn has been expanding its own manufacturing capacity in Malaysia,
Tainan, Mexico and the US, making its business model less asset-light than before. With the
server market still in the high growth period and the payout ratio remaining at around 60%, we
expect the favourable dividend policy to be sustained going forward.
Related party &
transaction 3
In 2025, revenue from related parties accounted for 0.2% of total revenue, while purchases from
related parties were 9.2% of cost of sales. The main purchase counterparty is Wistron (3231 TT;
Wiwynn's parent company), with purchases from Wistron accounting for 8.09% of total
procurement. While Wiwynn is a professional assembly vendor for data center servers, it still
outsources a portion of its production capacity to its parent company, Wistron. At the same time,
Wiwynn has expanded its own production bases in Malaysia, Tainan, Mexico and the US,
reducing its reliance on Wistron. This business model still causes meaningful transactions with
related parties, although the exposure has declined materially. Indeed, we are concerned
whether Wistron collects fair manufacturing fees from Wiwynn. As the company states that
trading terms of purchase transactions with related parties cannot be compared with third-party
vendors due to product specifications, we are not able to assess this concern.
S Supply chain management 1
Wiwynn focuses on providing various products and system solutions for hyperscale datacentres
and cloud infrastructures. There are over 100 suppliers providing key components, electronic
materials, and mechanical materials in Wiwynn’s supply chain. For the new joiners that target to
penetrate into Wiwynn’s supply chain, Wiwynn has formed the “Supplier Evaluation Group” to
conduct: 1) supplier management and supply capabilities evaluation, 2) technical capability
development assessment, and 3) partner quality assurance (these include mainly topics
undertaken by RBA, such as no child labour, no conflict minerals, no use of environmentally
hazardous substances, etc.). New suppliers are also required to be ISO9001, ISO14001, IECQ
QC 080000, and OHSAS 18001 (ISO 45001) qualified.
Wiwynn has strong supply chain risk management by conducting audits regularly. Apart from
considering the technical skills, quality date, delivery dates and price completeness of suppliers,
Wiwynn audits its suppliers to endeavour to protect the environment, ensure safety and health of
the environment, and emphasise the importance of human rights, based on Wiwynn’s CSR
Management System.
S Data security 2
To serve world-class cloud service providers, Wiwynn has been constantly improving its
information security management. Employees are educated on the protection of the product,
technology, and information privacy of customers.
In response to the global trends in data protection laws that tend to strengthen the security of
classified information, Wiwynn has mandated that all assigned PCs have the Windows Bitlocker
HDD encryption activated to avoid classified data being accessed without permission. In 2020, it
also introduced Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA). Beyond the security measures for hardware,
the company controls access to office, labs, and conference room in a robust manner to prevent
theft of the hardware.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

### Page 1

M
Update
ASMedia Technology Inc  | Asia Pacific
Inventory correction extends
into 1Q27
Inventory adjustment extends into 1Q27. Management sees improving
motherboard sell-through, but expects customer destocking to constrain shipments
in 4Q26–1Q27. AM4 exceeded 70% of chipset shipments in 3Q and should remain
dominant in 4Q, weighing on ASPs. Management indicated GM of around 50% in
4Q26–1Q27 as foundry and backend costs rise; alternative packaging/testing sources

should begin helping from 2Q27. October's stronger proprietary mix provided some
relief, but 2027 revenue guidance remains unfinalized, with 20% growth now
considered aggressive.
Recovery likely depends on execution from 2H27. ASMedia signed a new SOW
with the customer and currently sees itself as the sole supplier. Management hopes
shipments will start in 2Q27, with meaningful platform volume from 3Q27; ASPs
would be at least 50–60% above AM4. PCIe switches contributed 6–8% of revenue

after Gen4 yield and thermal issues delayed design-ins, versus a double-digit mix
target for 2027. USB4 is expected to more than double from 2026's depressed base,
although robotics remains small. New products can support recovery, but higher
development spending limits near-term operating leverage.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
ASMedia Technology Inc (5269.TW)
Base case, residual income model. Key assumptions: cost of equity of 9.8% (beta 1.3, risk-free
rate 2% and risk premium 6%), medium-term growth rate of 8.0%, payout ratio of 75% and
terminal growth rate of 5.2%.
Risks to Upside
n AMD gains market share thanks to better-than-expected performance.
n In-house designed products grow faster than expected.
n US-China tensions improve and US tariffs alleviate.

Risks to Downside
n AMD cannot get substrate supply, causing earnings downside for ASMedia.
n Intel adopts TSMC's technologies more aggressively to compete with AMD.
n Spec migration on USB 4 is slower.

ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

 결국 제자리를 찾아갈 것

hyungkeun.ryu@daishin.com

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 목표주가 500,000 
유지  
현재주가 
(26.10.29) 
231,000 
반도체업종

◼ 3분기 실적발표에 대한 시장의 반응은 다소 냉소적. 
◼ 주주환원은 시점의 문제일 뿐. 강화 방향성은 여전히 유효. 
◼ 메모리반도체 이익의 폭증이 가격의 매력을 유발. 비중확대 전략 유효.

매력적인 가격, 변하지 않은 주주환원 정책의 방향성 
목표주가와 매수의견 유지. 메모리반도체 주도 폭발적 이익 상승이 가격 (주
가)의 매력을 지속 유발하고 있고, 주주환원 정책의 강화 방향성에도 변한 것
은 없다는 판단. 절대적으로 싼 주가를 비중 확대의 기회로 삼을 필요. 
1. 주주환원, 시점의 문제 
금번 실적발표에 대한 시장의 반응은 다소 냉소적. 대외 불확실성이 큰 만큼, 
시장은 자사주 매입/소각과 같은 즉각적 주가 부양 정책의 발표를 기대.  
당사는 삼성전자의 주주환원이 “시점의 문제”라 생각. 총 주주환원의 규모도, 
차기 (2027-2029년) 주주환원 정책의 강화 방향에도 변한 것은 없다는 판
단. 시장이 기대하는 자사주 매입/소각은 우선주 중심으로 시행될 가능성이 
높으며, 시행 시, 그 규모는 25조원 내외일 것 (DPS 12,567원)으로 전망. 
2. 메모리반도체, 더 올라갈 것 
보수적 환율 전망 (2027년 평균 환율 1,300원 적용)에도, 2027년 메모리반도
체 영업이익은 608조원에 달할 것. 이익 전망의 상향 여력 또한 유효. 
1) 최대 생산, 최대 판매 
삼성전자는 금일 4Q26 DRAM 판매량 가이던스로 전분기 대비 10% 중후반 
성장 (q-q +18%로 추정)을 제시. 일부 투자자들은 높아진 기저 부담으로 
2027년 판매 성장이 제한될 것을 우려. 당사는 높아진 기저에도 2027년 
DRAM 판매가 전년대비 20% 상승할 것으로 전망. 
- 극단적 수급 불균형 속, 삼성전자는 “최대 생산, 최대 판매” 정책을 시행 
중. 삼성전자만의 강력한 Execution이 20% 대 성장을 유지시킬 원동력. 
- DRAM 1c 수율의 조기 개선 속, 범용 DRAM 양산에 DRAM 1c Capa가 일
부 투입될 것으로 예상. Bit 성장을 높일 수 있는 변화가 될 것. 
2) 열려 있는 가격의 상방 
1Q27 모바일 DRAM에서 Surprise가 발생할 것이라는 의견을 유지 (전분기 
대비 30% 이상 상승). 장기계약이 체결된 CSP향으로도 가격의 추가 상승 여
력이 존재한다는 점을 상기할 필요. CSP의 경우, 당장의 비용 부담보다 AI 시
장 선점을 우선시. 장기계약으로 형성된 가격 Band의 천장 (Ceiling) 또한 시
장 우려 대비 낮지 않음을 감안할 필요. 2027년 범용 DRAM ASP는 전년대비 
40% 중후반까지 상승할 가능성 (현 전망: 전년대비 34% 상승).  
3) HBM, 제품 경쟁력과 점유율의 동반 성장  
최근 삼성전자향으로 HBM4/HBM4e 추가 공급 요청이 쇄도하고 있는 것으
로 추정. 일부 고객은 연내 추가 공급 시 2027년 계약 가격을 선제 지급할 
의사를 표출. 제품 경쟁력이 회복되었음을 보여주는 증거라 생각. 
제품 경쟁력의 회복과 최대 Capa의 이점 속, 2027년은 HBM 점유율 1위를 
확보하는 기념비적 해가 될 것으로 전망 (매출액 기준 점유율 38% 예상).

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
26년 3분기 환율 부진에도 영업이익 100조는 넘긴다 
삼성전자 26년 3분기 실적은 이전 전망치를 하회할 전망이다. 매출액은 195.2조원으
로 이전 분기 대비 13.8% 증가하고, 영업이익은 101.7조원으로 13.6% 증가할 것으
로 예상한다. 영업이익률은 52.1%로 전 분기 대비 0.1%p 하락할 전망이다. 원/달러 
환율 영향이 주요 변수로 작용한 것으로 추정한다. 2분기에 집행되지 않았던 성과급 
일부가 3분기에 반영된 영향도 있을 것으로 예상한다. DS 부문은 매출액 149.9조원, 
영업이익은 101.8조원으로 추정하고, 디스플레이 매출액 8.8조원, 영업이익 0.9조
원, MX/네트워크는 매출액 34.3조원, 영업적자 1.1조원으로 예상한다. VD/가전도 
영업적자 규모가 커질 것으로 예상한다.  
주주환원은 주가 상승을 견인할 것 
8월 21일 이후 주주환원에 대한 관심이 커졌다. 자사주 규모는 크지 않겠지만 배당
을 통한 주주환원 규모는 사상 최대 규모일 것으로 예상한다. 글로벌 대표 IT업체와 
비교해도 손색없는 수준이다. 메모리 업체의 주주환원 규모가 100조원 넘게 집행된 
적이 없어서 주가에 어떠한 영향을 미칠 지가 관전 포인트인데 긍정적 역할을 할 것
으로 기대한다. 자사주를 통한 EPS 개선은 아니지만, 안정적이고 지속적인 규모의 
주주환원은 주가를 한층 더 높은 수준에서 평가 받는 계기가 될 것으로 판단한다.  
현저한 저평가 구간, 이전 전망 유지 
삼성전자에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 메모리 공급 부족 상황이 장기화될 
것으로 전망하고, 이로 인해 수익성은 현재 수준에서 유지되고, 이익 규모는 안정적
으로 성장할 것으로 기대하고, 현저히 저평가 국면에 있다고 판단하기 때문이다. 목
표주가는 460,000원을 유지한다.  
목표주가 (유지) 460,000원  
현재가 (10/1) 276,000원

### Page 4

삼성전자 (005930)

4 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
반도체 Check Point 
1. 차별화된 주주환원 전략. 배경은 안정적 이익 규모 
2026년에는 대대적인 규모의 주주환원이 예상된다. 100조원 내외 수준으로 
예상하는데 상당 부분은 배당으로 집행될 것으로 추정한다. 배당성향이 25%가 유지될 
것으로 예상하기 때문이다. 자사주 매입 여력은 20조원 내외 수준일 것으로 예상한다. 
주주환원은 주가 밸류 상승에 기여할 것으로 기대한다. 일회성이 아니고 장기적으로 
높은 수준의 주주환원이 가능하다는 것은 싸이클 변동에 따른 이익의 변동성이 
낮다는 의미이기도 하다. 
2. AI 모델 개발 속도 조절. 그래도 메모리 수요를 줄일 수는 없다 
Anthropic CEO는 현재 AI 개발 속도가 지나치게 빨라서 인간의 능력을 뛰어 넘는 
임계점에 이를 가능성이 높아져서 개발 속도를 늦출 필요가 있다고 언급했다. 이를 
Meta, Google, SpaceX 수장들이 공감했다. 우선 이들의 공감으로 AI 모델의 개발 
속도가 둔화될 것으로 보고 있지 않다. 또한, 이를 가정하더라도 향후 AI 모델은 
Agent AI로 진화할 것으로 보고 있다. 이에 따른 메모리에 대한 필요성은 급격히 
증가할 것으로 기대한다. 
3. 자신감 넘치는 HBM  
HBM4가 2026년 하반기부터 주력 제품이 될 것으로 전망한다. 26년 3분기에는 
비중이 40% 내외, 4분기에는 60%를 상회할 것으로 예상한다. 연초 기대 대비로는 
낮은 수준이다. 삼성전자의 점유율만 보면 연초 대비 높은 수준의 점유율이 예상된다.  
HBM4 물량이 예상보다 늦게 본격화되면서 2026년 HBM 시장 규모는 이전 전망 
대비 축소될 전망이다. HBM4도 늦은 출시로 연내 생산 물량은 이전 전망치인 130억 
Gb을 하회할 것으로 예상한다.  
2027년 HBM4 가격이 최근에 확정이 되었고, 이를 통해서 DRAM 수준의 수익성 
확보가 가능할 것으로 기대한다. 수익성 발목을 잡았던 HBM은 2027년 물량부터는 
영업이익 개선의 변수가 될 것으로 전망한다.

장기적 주주환원은 
안정적 이익이 뒷받침 
Agent AI는 메모리 
수요를 증가시킨다 
HBM4 안정적 진입한 
것으로 판단

Document 261: AI バブル測定器としての鉱工業生産（26 年９月）

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI バブル測定器としての鉱工業生産（26 年９月）.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

2026 年 10 月 30 日(金)

1/5 
AI バブル測定器としての鉱工業生産（26 年９月） 
 経済調査部主席エコノミスト藤代宏一 
（℡：050-5474-6123）

・筆者が日本経済と株式市場の行方を読む上で定点観測している日本の鉱工業生産は、2026年
９月に前月比＋2.2％となり、市場予想（同＋1.3％）を上回った。経産産業省の予測補正値
（同＋1.6％）に対しても上振れた。増産は３ヶ月ぶり。８月が速報段階の同▲1.7％から確
報で同▲2.4％へと下方修正されていたため、９月の増産で前月までの落ち込みを概ね取り戻
した格好となる。もっとも、生産水準は直近最高だった６月の104.6を上回るには至っておら
ず、数量ベースの生産活動は、経済産業省の判断通り「一進一退」となっている。

・日経平均株価は先行き 12 ヶ月 72,000 円程度で推移するだろう 
・USD/JPYは先行き 12ヶ月 155円程度で推移するだろう 
・日銀は政策金利を 12月に 1.5％とした後、27年末までに 2.25％とするだろう 
・FEDはFF 金利を、年内は 4.0％で据え置くだろう

### Page 2

2/5 
・増産となったのは８業種。上位３業種は自動車工業、生産用機械工業、汎用・業務機械工業
であった。反対に減産の寄与度が大きかった３業種は鉄鋼・非鉄金属、窯業・土石製品工
業、化学工業（除く無機・有機化学工業・医薬品）であった。７月に熊本で発生した地震に
よって８月までは自動車生産などに影響が出ていたが、その挽回生産が９月の数値を押し上
げる格好となった。

・10月初旬に実施された生産予測調査に基づけば、製造工業の生産計画は10月に前月比＋5.1％
となった後、11月は同＋0.8％と増産が見込まれている。経産省経済解析室が生産予測指数の
上振れバイアスを補正した10月の予測値は同＋3.9％とかなり強い。電子部品・デバイス工業
と生産用機械工業で大幅な増産が見込まれているほか、自動車の挽回生産も続く見込みであ
る。生産活動全体の方向感を決める輸送機械工業は９月（実績）に前月比＋5.5％となった
後、10月（計画）に同＋0.9％、11月に同＋4.3％となっている。日本や米国における自動車
販売台数が堅調に推移していることを踏まえると、基調的に弱含んでいく展開は想定しにく
い。また生産活動の先行指標として有用な製造業ＰＭＩが４月以降に有意な上昇を示してい
ることから判断すると、製造業全体としてはＡＩ・半導体需要が浸透する形で増勢が強まっ
ていくと期待される。10月製造業ＰＭＩは53.4と高水準を維持している。

・なお、鉱工業生産指数は生産の「数量」に重きを置いて生産高を計測する統計であり、「付
加価値」を直接捕捉する性格のものではない。例えば、高付加価値を狙った品質向上によっ
て生産数量・重量が減少する場合、統計上はそれを減産と見做し、付加価値を過小評価して
しまうこともある。したがって、鉱工業生産統計が示すほど日本経済（製造業）の付加価値

### Page 3

3/5 
創出力が停滞していない可能性を念頭に置く必要がある。事実、過去数年は付加価値の合計
である実質ＧＤＰと鉱工業生産指数の水準・方向感の乖離が大きくなっている。

・株式市場における重要度が高い電子部品・デバイス工業については、前月比＋3.6％の増産で
あった。地震の影響もあってか、ＩＣの増産に一服感がみられる一方、コンデンサや電子回
路基板などで増産の動きがみられる。生産用機械工業に分類される半導体製造装置は同＋
3.4％の増産となり、３ヶ月平均値でみても＋7.7％と力強い増産基調にある。生産予測調査
に目を向けると、電子部品・デバイス工業は10月に同＋16.6％、11月に同▲1.7％と均してみ
れば大幅な増産が計画されている。半導体製造装置が含まれる生産用機械工業は10月に同＋
15.5％と大幅増産となった後、11月は同▲5.7％と減産が見込まれているが、こちらも基調は
強い。個別企業の業績予想などから判断しても、先行きは半導体製造装置の増産がかなりの
高確度で期待できる。関連指標の機械受注統計における「電子計算機等」の受注額水準も鈍
化する気配に乏しい。この指標は日経平均株価との連動性がある。

### Page 4

4/5

・電子部品・デバイス工業の動向を仔細にみると、９月は出荷が前年比＋0.8％へと減速するな
か、在庫は同▲6.1％へとマイナス幅を拡大した。この結果、出荷・在庫バランス（両者の前
年比差分から算出）は＋6.9pとなり、３ヶ月平均でも＋7.0ptとはっきりとしたプラス領域を
維持した。引き続き、製品需給が引き締まる方向にあることが示唆されている。ここで電子
部品・デバイス工業の出荷・在庫バランスと、日経平均株価が長期的に連動性を有してきた
事実を再確認したい。株価指数において半導体を直接手掛ける企業の存在感は必ずしも大き
くはないものの、半導体製造装置、電子部品、化学品など「広義半導体」で見ればその存在
感は大きく、結果的に日本株全体のうねりを作り出す構図があると筆者は理解している。ち
なみに出荷在庫バランス株価は方向感こそ一致しているものの、両者のかい離は大きくなっ
ている。その要因として、数量ベースの出荷・在庫バランスには、メモリなど製品価格の高
騰が反映されないことが考えられる。

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。

・最後に先行きの出荷・在庫バランスを見極めるために在庫循環図の位置取りを確認すると、2 
025年は中心点付近で渦を描いてきたが、2026年入り後は右方向への動きが明確化しつつあ
る。過去の経験則に従えば、今後は右上領域（在庫増・出荷増）に向けて歩み出すと予想さ
れる。

Document 281: Blue Wave_ Democrats sweep the US midterms

Source PDF: all report pdf/Blue Wave_ Democrats sweep the US midterms.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

Blue Wave: Democrats 
sweep the US midterms  
Summary 
Event: The Democrats gained control of the House of 
Representatives and the Senate at the US midterm elections 
that took place this week.  
Our View: With the US government now no longer under 
unified control of the Republicans, gridlock has returned to 
Washington. We believe investors should not overreact to 
these results and should stay invested. 
In equities, investors should continue to diversify their 
equity exposure into more defensive sectors. As we 
transition to mid-cycle for the equity markets, we expect 
stocks to continue to deliver positive returns in the 8-10% 
range per annum, a downshift from the torrid pace of gains in 
the earlier part of the cycle.  
In bonds, income remains attractive given recent spread 
widening. We favour non-hyperscaler investment grade 
corporate bonds at the three to seven-year part of the 
curve. We continue to expect the US dollar to remain range-
bound in the near term, as we expect two more rate hikes 
from the Fed, but as this mini rate hike cycle ends in the 
second quarter of 2027, the USD index (DXY) is likely to 
depreciate towards 98.5 as markets start to anticipate a Fed 
rate cut in the second half of next year.  
Oil is likely to rebound in the short-term on heightened 
geopolitical risk, but as supply chains adapt to the current 
situation and Congress exercises oversight on defense 
spending, WTI prices are likely to drift towards our 12-month 
target of USD 80 per barrel.

Democrats made solid gains across the Senate, House 
of Representatives and State Governors 
Results reported as at 7:45PM Singapore Time

Source: The Wall Street Journal, Standard Chartered

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 5 November 2026

Major implications of Democrat control of 
Congress   
1) Fiscal expansionary policies may slow. Democrats are 
now in position to block any new tax cuts or spending plans 
from the Trump administration. At the same time, any fiscal 
policies proposed by Democrats may also face Trump’s veto. 
Hence the fiscal trajectory of the US will likely remain stuck 
over the next two years. As we get into the second half of 
2027, the US debt ceiling will once again loom, and gridlock 
could potentially bring back government shutdowns. 
However, as they have always done in the past, compromise 
will ultimately prevail, in our opinion.  
2) Trump remains in control of foreign policy. With the 
midterms now out of the way, could Trump decide to re-start 
combat operations with Iran? We believe there is a risk that 
US and Israel – now that both countries’ have concluded 
important elections – will once again be tempted to restart 
combat operations to force an end to the conflict. However, 
the duration of any renewed hostilities may be limited by 
Congress next year as the Democrats will likely withhold 
funding for further military operations. In the near-term, oil 
prices may experience renewed volatility before stabilising at 
a lower level next year. The introduction of new tariffs on 
trading partners may prove harder with Democrat oversight. 
3) AI data centre moratoriums could grow, though model 
development regulatory changes would be harder to 
pass. While the headlines have focused on the House and 
Senate results, one important development has also 
occurred at the gubernatorial level. Notably, Democrats have 
won the governor races in Texas and Georgia, two big data 
centre states. As data centre build out permits are largely 
decided at the local state level, this will have important 
ramifications for build out plans next year. However, any 
Democratic proposals to regulate AI model developments 
would need to be signed off by President Trump, who 
continues to wield veto power on any such proposals.   
Asset class strategy 
Equities: Remain invested and continue to diversify.  
While investors initially overreacted, President Trump’s 
relatively conciliatory response has provided some welcome 
relief, helping markets recover strongly from their pre-market 
lows. Hence, we recommend investors avoid 
overreacting, remain invested and continue to diversify 
their equity exposure across sectors and regions.  
A few themes stand out from the early pre-market reaction to 
the mid-term elections. On the positive side, defensive stocks 
continue to fare well, while themes favoured by Democrats 
are also outperforming. Clean energy is a notable 
beneficiary, with solar stocks among the biggest winners

Policy uncertainty and a change in the innovation cycle 
is consistent with our view of a mid-cycle transition, 
where equity returns moderate but remain positive

Standard Chartered Bank

today. Affordability-related themes are also rallying, including 
companies that could benefit from potential tariff rollbacks. 
Medicaid-related stocks are also moving higher on 
expectations that potential policy changes could provide a 
tailwind for the healthcare sector. 
On the negative side, semiconductor weakness continues 
amid concerns around potential data centre moratoriums. 
Our AI capex estimates remain conservative relative to 
consensus, but with a potential reset in expectations around 
data centre spending, investors should remain selective. 
After downgrading semiconductors last month, we have 
maintained a conservative stance on the industry, with a 
lower recommended allocation compared to September. 
While we believe the mid-term election outcome is not a 
complete surprise to markets, particularly given pre-election 
polling and the recent weakness in both the S&P 500 and 
Nasdaq, the overnight correction highlights how complacent 
investors had become, with the VIX initially surging more than 
10% in pre-market trading, although down from intra-day 
highs after President Trump’s comments. 
As we have highlighted in our recent notes on the transition 
to mid-cycle, this phase is typically characterised by greater 
political headwinds. The mid-term election outcome 
reinforces the trend. The experience of 2015-2017, 
highlighted in the second table, offers a useful historical 
parallel. Despite the knee-jerk reaction, investors should not 
overreact based on one day’s moves. While we had trimmed 
equities to a core allocation last week, we continue to expect 
equities to deliver mid-to-high single-digit returns per annum 
during the mid-cycle, even amid political headwinds, 
supported by steady underlying earnings growth in both 2027 
and 2028. 
So, what should investors do to navigate the next few months 
before the newly elected representatives are sworn in and 
take control of the House and Senate? As we have been 
highlighting in recent notes, equity markets should still deliver 
positive returns, albeit at a more moderate pace. Hence, we 
recommend staying invested but diversification remains key 
to navigating the next phase of the cycle.  
FX: DXY near-term support at 100.7, 12-month target at 
98.5. A Democratic sweep potentially removes the legislative 
runway for further tariff escalation and for fiscal expansion, 
the two structural pillars of US exceptionalism that have 
anchored dollar demand in 2026. Reduced fiscal impulse 
compresses the growth and yield differential that has kept the 
US dollar well-bid all year. Additionally, potential Democratic 
restrictions on AI and data centre investment could weigh on 
US equity outperformance, which is likely to act as a 
secondary dollar-negative channel.  
However, despite historical readings supporting a near-term 
DXY headwind from a Democratic sweep, the magnitude will

Fixed Income: Still attractive all-in yields; prefer 
investment grade corporates at three-to-seven year 
tenors to earn income and manage interest rate risk. The 
US Treasury bond market reaction appears to reflect a 
moderation in expectations of future economic growth after 
the election results. Yields at the intermediate end of the 
curve dropped the most, while the 30-year US yield 
moderated more modestly. We continue to expect two more

inflation and the robust economy support further tightening.  
In the past few weeks, the US Treasury yield curve has 
broadly shifted downwards, after our opportunistic call to go 
long 10-year Treasuries at the end of September. We are of 
the view the ultra-long end downshift in yields is overdone 
(given the still challenging US fiscal trajectory) and would 
recommend investors who had earlier taken long duration 
exposures at the 20 to 30-year parts of the curve reduce their 
positions now. We continue to believe the 3 to 7-year part of 
the curve offers the most optimal balance of income and 
interest rate risk, particularly via investment corporate bonds. 
Within corporate credit, we expect a potentially slower pace 
of AI data centre buildouts could marginally improve market 
technicals, as hyperscaler and tech sector bond issuance 
may slow (and reduce “crowding out” of other bond 
issuances). Conversely, from a credit perspective, we believe 
this dynamic may also make the data centre focused deals 
less viable, and would be cautious on some of this sector’s 
lower quality names. Meanwhile, less AI capex dependent 
issuers continue to offer solid credit profiles amidst the still 
strong economy. High yield corporate spreads have widened 
in recent weeks but dispersion within the complex is wide, 
with BBs and Bs credit spreads staying relatively resilient, 
while CCCs have widened to distressed levels. For income-
focused investors, we would recommend staying within the 
crossover range of between BBB-BB.  
Commodities: As noted in the opening section, we believe 
there is a higher near-term risk of a restart of combat 
operations in Iran given the conclusion of the midterm 
elections. Given that, we believe there is a risk that oil 
prices could see a short-term spike, despite recent 
diplomatic overtures from Iran. However, for 2027 we 
continue to expect oil to stabilise at lower levels, as we 
think the Mideast situation should de-escalate given 
Democratic oversight of the defense budget, and the likely re-
configuration of oil supply chains. On the margin, we are also 
closely monitoring regulatory developments in the US shale

### Page 1

COP31 Preview: Cooperation Amid Fragmentation
COP31, taking place in Antalya, Türkiye from 9 to 20 November 2026, will test whether the ‘Implementation COP’ can
turn climate ambition into finance delivery amid heightened geopolitical tensions. Corporates and investors should
watch for signals that could shape policy and capital allocation, including commitments to grid investment,
electrification, adaptation and carbon markets. We also expect AI’s climate potential and rising electricity demand to

Deutsche Bank
Research
Debbie Jones | Andrew Dixon
S U S T A I N A B I L I T Y  | T H E M A T I C  R E S E A R C H  | O C T O B E R  2 0 2 6
The report outlines the top 10 focus topics, discussions and outcomes for COP31 (see pages 1-3 for a summary)

### Page 2

C O P 3 1  P r ev iew  |  TO P  1 0  (1  o f 3 )
Antalya  - 10 things to watch for at COP31
2 Debbie Jones  |  COP31 Preview  |  October 2026
From 9 to 20 November, ~100,000 participants and nearly 200 Parties are expected at COP31 in Antalya, Türkiye. This is an ‘Implementation COP’ and will focus on climate finance delivery for
mitigation and adaptation commitments. Yes, there will be geopolitical tensions and competitiveness concerns. But, for corporates and investors, the discussions will shape how private capital is

mobilised, and how policies will affect de-risking efforts. We expect electrification efforts will feature prominently, driven by energy security concerns as well as by rising AI-driven power
demand. Furthermore, AI’s ability to help tackle climate change has the potential to play a bigger role in shaping the agenda of future COPs.
01
TÜRKIYE HOSTS, AUSTRALIA LEADS
NEGOTIATIONS
Türkiye and Australia must stay aligned
to drive progress
See page 6
– As host, Türkiye’s Murat Kurum will preside over COP31 and its Action Agenda, which is intended to build cooperation between

willing participants to drive climate action. Australia’s Chris Bowen will run the formal negotiations that will seek consensus (or
lack of objection) on finance delivery and existing climate commitments. Feedback following pre-COP meetings on the
arrangement has been broadly positive, but concern remains over how any potential disagreement will be handled.
– This structure is the result of a compromise after both countries bid to host. Both countries want COP31 to focus on climate

finance implementation and clean-energy growth, however, they remain coal-reliant (in different ways) with no national coal
phase-out date, a criticism likely to continue from climate advocates at COP.
02
CLIMATE FINANCE
Success at COP31 requires notable
progress on mobilising existing
commitments
See page 7
– For COP31 to be successful it will need to deliver agreements to mobilise and channel climate finance. For context, at COP29 in
2024 it was agreed (as part of formal negotiations) that developed nations would commit $300bn in climate finance annually

by 2035, with a call for $1.3tn from all sources. At present, more credible plans are needed to secure existing commitments
and attract broader capital. Note the EU has already stated it will not make new finance pledges at COP31.
– Article 9 contains the main financing provisions of the Paris Agreement and has driven much debate during COP talks.
Developing countries want focus on public funding from developed governments (Article 9.1), while developed countries prefer

to discuss Article 9 in a broader context. This allows private capital to be included. COP30 launched a two-year work
programme on Article 9, but June’s Bonn talks stalled, so COP31 meetings will attempt to reach agreement.
03
ADAPTATION & RESILIENCE
Implications of a 1.5°C overshoot point
to more adaptation financing
See pages 8–9
– The UN has warned that an overshoot of the Paris Agreement’s 1.5°C goal is now unavoidable, at least temporarily. That has

dampened morale for climate supporters ahead of COP but, at the same time, it has drawn more attention to the topic of
adaptation finance, which is set to be on the formal negotiating agenda. Parties appear deadlocked over whether the COP30
goal to triple adaptation finance by 2035 (potential annual target of $120bn) should be explicitly referenced in the final COP31
decision on the Global Goal on Adaptation (GGA). This is important, as anchoring it to the GGA could signal finance flows.

– The EU Commission proposed the Climate Resilience law on 27 October. It needs Parliament and Council approval, but if
approved, member states will be required to run national climate-risk assessments based on a 3°C-by-2100 warming scenario
(i.e. the EU is planning for overshoot). Members would also need to adopt resilience plans, including details of costs and
funding, with The Commission estimating the EU needs €70bn a year for adaptation to 2050, vs. €29bn of current spend.

### Page 3

Antalya  - 10 things to watch for at COP31
3 Debbie Jones  |  COP31 Preview  |  October 2026
04
ELECTRIFICATION
Türkiye to champion voluntary
electrification commitments
See page 10
– Türkiye's voluntary Action Agenda includes a ‘35-by-35’ target to lift electricity’s share of final energy use from ~23% to 35% by
2035, alongside waste, building energy intensity and circularity targets. This sits outside the formal negotiations and commits
only those that volunteer. China and the EU have voiced support, and the timing is right given global energy-security concerns,

though some question the emissions impact if fossil fuels are used to generate the additional electricity.
06
TRADE TENSIONS
Trade’s inclusion in the provisional
agenda is contentious, with the
potential for disagreement over trade to
stall progress on wider agreements
See page 12
– The role of trade at COP is contentious, but China wants it discussed. On 21 October, Vice Minister Li Gao called ‘unilateralism
and protectionism’ a ‘new problem’ undermining collective climate efforts. Specifically, he wants COP31 to discuss the free

flow of green technology. This was widely read as being aimed at US and EU tariffs on Chinese cleantech.
– Despite a COP30 agreement to create three annual trade dialogues in 2026–28 (the first formal space under the UNFCCC to
discuss trade) away from COP, developing countries are still pushing for trade to be a formal negotiating item in Antalya, and it
is on the provisional agenda. They argue that climate-related trade measures such as the CBAM burden their exporters and

question whether they are compatible with UNFCCC principles. The EU meanwhile claims they help prevent carbon leakage,
with developed countries preferring trade discussions to focus on technical information-sharing without duplicating the WTO.
C O P 3 1  P r ev iew  |  TO P  1 0  (2  o f 3 )
07
GEOPOLITICS & MULTILATERALISM
Consensus under strain, but ‘coalitions
of the willing’ are rising
See pages 13–14
– Geopolitical fragmentation and the US exit from the Paris Agreement make consensus, historically central to COP decision-

making, harder among nearly 200 nations. This has encouraged more voluntary action and “coalitions of the willing” to pursue
more ambitious goals. If these strategies deliver more effective progress, their approach will shape future COPs.
05
FOSSIL FUELS
Phase-out likely not on the formal
agenda, but workarounds are emerging
See page 11
– Brazil’s COP30 presidency is to present a fossil fuel transition roadmap separately, outside the UN formal negotiating process.

For perspective, COP28 in Dubai reached consensus on “transitioning away from fossil fuels in energy systems”, but at COP30
in Belém (2025) calls from more than 80 countries for a transition roadmap faced opposition from other nations, so it was
omitted from the final decision and moved to a separate track.
– Santa Marta is a second workaround for the lack of formal agreement on a transition roadmap. The Colombia- and
Netherlands-led process is a ‘coalition of the willing’ of 57 participants (excluding the US, China, India and Russia) agreeing to

voluntary action to move away from fossil fuels faster than consensus allows. At COP31, Colombia and the Netherlands want
to bring the Santa Marta outcomes into the summit. The format is not yet settled, but the Australian and Turkish presidencies
have said they are open to it. The process then heads to a second conference in Tuvalu, co-hosted with Ireland, in 2027.

### Page 4

09
NATURE & CARBON CREDITS
Efforts to boost demand and build
credibility to help scale carbon markets
See page 16
– Carbon credit discussions will centre on government policies to boost demand and corporate engagement, with much of the
formal UN architecture now in place. If carbon markets can scale, the benefits would be wide-ranging (e.g. supporting
emissions targets and providing finance for developing countries). Credibility and greenwashing concerns remain key risks,

however; we expect prices to diverge in favour of high-integrity credits.
– The Tropical Forest Forever Facility (TFFF), launched at COP30, plans a fixed-income portfolio to reward verified forest
conservation. It now has ~$7.8bn of sponsor capital against a $10bn end-2026 target. In our view, fresh COP31 commitments
would signal appetite to fund nature conservation and protection.
10
PACIFIC, OCEANS & SEA-LEVEL RISE
Australia to bring Pacific priorities into

focus
See page 17
– Australia asked for a Pacific pre-COP in return for ceding the hosting. It has promised to keep championing Pacific interests at
COP31. The Taku Pakasoa Declaration was adopted at that pre-COP on 6 October. ‘Taku Pakasoa’ is Tuvaluan for strength
found in unity and collective action. It is a joint call to keep 1.5°C alive, calling for action on climate finance, resilience and
oceans. Its intention is to give the Pacific a shared position going into COP31 and signal its policy priorities to other blocs.

Other countries are invited to sign on. The climate-ocean nexus will also be a COP31 focus, including investable solutions.
COP31 should indicate what COP can deliver in a more fragmented geopolitical world. Key questions? Can the current structure help mobilise meaningful finance and investment, and do
smaller coalitions and voluntary commitments deliver meaningful outcomes? Answers here could shape future COP agendas.  Looking ahead, Ethiopia will host COP32 in 2027. There, we

expect a continued focus on delivering on commitments and improving access to climate finance, reflecting African priorities. COP33 then falls to Asia-Pacific in 2028, with China
reportedly considering a bid and South Korea potentially interested.
We should note that the second Global Stocktake, the Paris Agreement’s five-yearly review of collective progress, begins at COP31 and concludes at COP33, with a technical assessment
(2027) informing the final negotiations. Its outcome should inform countries’ next national climate plans (NDCs, their emissions-reduction pledges), due in 2030.

08
AI & CLIMATE
AI’s power demand in focus, with the
Antalya pledge calling for low emissions
infrastructure
See page 15
– The Antalya AI Pledge invites governments and companies to commit voluntarily to sustainable infrastructure and
environmental transparency. Its call for zero- and low-emissions energy infrastructure leaves room for nuclear power,
prompting discussion of its role in meeting data-centre demand alongside renewables. While we see binding commitments as

unlikely, watch for corporate power commitments and technology partnerships.
– We expect AI’s climate potential and rising electricity demand to feature prominently at COP31, prompting debate over data-
centre energy use. We also expect AI to help delegations manage COP’s heavy paperwork, from reviewing negotiating texts
and submissions to comparing successive draft decisions. This should help negotiating teams prepare more effectively and
make decisions faster, particularly smaller delegations with limited staff and resources.

### Page 6

TO P I C  1 | COP31’s UNIQUE STRUCTURE
COP31’s unique dual structure will need to bridge divides among negotiating groups and build consensus
As host, Türkiye’s Murat Kurum will preside over COP31 and its Action Agenda. Australia’s Chris Bowen will run the formal negotiations. This dual structure is the result of a compromise after
both countries bid to host. Feedback post pre-COP meetings on the unique arrangement has been broadly positive, but concern remains over how any disagreement between the two countries

would be handled should it occur. Both countries clearly want this year’s COP to focus on climate finance implementation and clean-energy growth.
A unique leadership structure
COP31 will take place in Antalya, Türkiye. The setup is unusual, emerging as part of a deal
between Türkiye and Australia after both countries bid to host the event. What began as a
compromise could become an advantage, provided the two countries remain aligned. Their
differing diplomatic relationships could help them bridge divides between negotiating groups

and build common ground on climate action and finance.
How it will work
Murat Kurum, Türkiye’s Minister of the Environment, will serve as COP31 President, overseeing
the conference and Action Agenda. Australia’s Minister for Climate Change and Energy, Chris
Bowen, will serve as President of Negotiations, including drafting final texts, with authority to
steer government-to-government talks. Pre-COP preparations have reportedly been positive.
They will, however, both likely face criticism from climate advocates asking for a plan to shift

from fossil fuels, notably coal, given efforts to position themselves as global climate leaders.
The Action Agenda includes targets for electrification, waste, buildings and circularity
This voluntary Agenda proposes 2035 targets of a 35% electricity share of final energy use (up
from 23% currently), halving projected municipal waste growth, cutting building energy
intensity by at least 25% and reaching at least 15% circular material use. While pledges are

non-binding, they can signal policy and decisions that could support infrastructure investment.
Formal negotiations to focus on implementing existing commitments
Formal negotiations seek consensus (or lack of objection) on implementation pathways. The
key need is guidance on how agreed climate-finance commitments will be funded and
delivered, and on countries’ roles. Adaptation will also be a focus, with countries aiming to
reach agreement on how to advance the Global Goal on Adaptation after discussions stalled

earlier this year. These formal talks remain separate from the voluntary Action Agenda.
Sources: COP31 Action Agenda, IEA and Eco-Business
